## Semantic Chunks
### Section: Introduction
- **Level**: `intro`
- **Hash**: `b0eee103caab6fd3a55a9c69394c301c92f6575bb200f8c3c761d514f677d26a`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-should-teachers-handle-ai-use-when-the-school-has-no-ai-policy` (Similarity: 0.9387)

```text
A vague or outdated academic integrity policy is the root cause of most AI-related disputes: when expectations are ambiguous, students make assumptions that lead to unintended misconduct.

Simply stating "No cheating with AI" in a syllabus is no longer sufficient in an era where AI is integrated into word processors, search engines, and grammar checkers. A modern, defensible classroom AI policy must explicitly define what tools are covered, delineate permitted vs. prohibited use cases, establish mandatory disclosure rules, require essay writing playback tracking , and guarantee student due process.

Below is a comprehensive blueprint detailing the 6 essential components every classroom AI policy must include, complete with copy-and-paste syllabus templates.

Checkmark Plagiarism helps educators enforce syllabus policies by pairing essay writing playback with AI detection , plagiarism detection , autograding , and integrations with Canvas and Google Classroom .
```

### Section: The 6 Essential Components of a Classroom AI Policy
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `5bc3d6c429bc45abede31ee99d0461928e7d6a78bdab474eb939b78f5b23c7b5`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-should-teachers-handle-ai-use-when-the-school-has-no-ai-policy` (Similarity: 0.8889)

```text
1. Explicit Scope & Definitions

Clearly define what constitutes AI in your course: large language models (ChatGPT, Claude), automated spinners (QuillBot), and generative grammar tools.

2. Permitted vs. Prohibited Boundaries

Specify exact use cases: e.g., brainstorming ideas (permitted with disclosure) vs. generating sentences or outlines (strictly prohibited).

3. Mandatory Disclosure Requirements

Establish a required AI Disclosure Appendix where students must list the tools, model versions, and exact prompts used for any assisted work.

4. Writing Process Tracking

Mandate that all essays be composed directly within the LMS editor with writing playback enabled to verify keystroke drafting.

5. Evidentiary Standards & Due Process

Guarantee that no student will be disciplined based solely on a detector score, and outline the right to an oral conference.

6. Progressive Consequence Framework

Define tiered consequences: educational rewriting for a first minor infraction vs. formal academic integrity referral for intentional fraud.
```

### Section: Copy-and-Paste Syllabus Policy Template
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `cf641d4963a42e91bfaa0b6c69279c38a4dfc76cbf2c2454215df13a4813d122`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-do-passage-level-ai-confidence-sliders-prevent-false-accusations-in-hybrid-student-drafts` (Similarity: 0.9162)

```text
Comprehensive Course Syllabus AI Policy:

Artificial Intelligence & Academic Integrity Policy: 
 1. Authorship Expectation: Unless explicitly stated on a specific assignment prompt, all writing, analysis, arguments, and citations in this course must be 100% authored by the enrolled student. Submitting text generated or paraphrased by AI models (such as ChatGPT, Claude, or QuillBot) constitutes authorship fraud and a violation of the academic honor code. 
 2. Process Verification: All major essays must be drafted within our LMS document editor with writing playback enabled. Submissions showing zero keystroke history, minimal active drafting duration, or unexplained wholesale paste events will require an oral defense before receiving credit. 
 3. Permitted Brainstorming & Disclosure: If an assignment permits AI for brainstorming or research exploration, you must include an AI Usage Appendix at the end of your paper detailing: (a) tool name and version, (b) prompts entered, and (c) your critical reflection on how you developed the ideas in your own words. 
 4. Investigation & Due Process: Academic integrity inquiries will never rely on automated detector scores alone. Inquiries are evaluated through multi-signal evidence, including writing playback timelines, citation database verification, historical writing baselines, and private student conferences.
```

### Section: A 5-Step Policy Implementation Checklist for Educators
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `321b9861a3c595f2b8b4d46f865179bce8a55b92c37158ec81233822cf577bf2`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-should-a-schools-ai-misconduct-policy-include` (Similarity: 0.8970)

```text
Educator Policy Implementation Protocol:

1. Publish the comprehensive AI policy in your syllabus and review it on Day 1. 
 2. Label every assignment prompt clearly: Level 1 (No AI), Level 2 (Assisted), or Level 3 (Co-Creation). 
 3. Require writing playback tracking directly inside Canvas or Google Classroom. 
 4. Provide a standard AI Disclosure Appendix template for all permitted assisted tasks. 
 5. Follow a multi-signal investigation protocol before making any academic integrity referral.
```
